<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove exponential decay of two-point correlations for the classical nearest-neighbor $`O(n)`$ model on the square lattice, for every <i>n</i> ≥ 3 and every finite positive inverse temperature. The estimate is uniform over finite free-boundary subgraphs and bounded nonnegative edge strengths. This resolves positively the all-temperature exponential spin-decay conjecture for these models.

- **claim-uniform-spin-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $n\ge3$ and every finite $\beta>0$, there are constants $A=A(n,\beta)<\infty$ and $m=m(n,\beta)>0$ such that, for every finite nearest-neighbor square-lattice subgraph $G$, every strength array $b\in[0,\beta]^E$, and all $x,y\in V$, $$0\le \langle\sigma_x\cdot\sigma_y\rangle_{G,b}^{(n)} \le A\exp(-m\\|x-y\\|_2). \label{eq:main-bound}$$
  - Explanation: The spin correlation is nonnegative and decays exponentially in distance, uniformly over finite square-lattice subgraphs and bounded ferromagnetic strength arrays. The constants may depend on the spin dimension and inverse temperature.
- **claim-free-boundary-limit-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-limits)): Let $\Lambda_L=[-L,L]^2\cap\mathbb Z^2$, with all its nearest-neighbor edges, free boundary conditions, and constant strength $\beta>0$. For every $n\ge3$ and $x\in\mathbb Z^2$, $$\limsup_{L\to\infty} \left\|\langle\sigma_0\cdot\sigma_x\rangle_{\Lambda_L,\beta}^{(n)}\right\| \le A e^{-m\\|x\\|_2}, \label{eq:limsup}$$ with the constants of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Every subsequential local weak limit of these laws satisfies the same two-point bound.
  - Explanation: The same spin-correlation estimate passes to the volume limsup and to every subsequential local weak limit of the free-boundary square measures. This does not assert uniqueness of the limiting law.
- **claim-gradient-response-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gradient)): If $f:V\to\mathbb C$ vanishes on $P$, then, for $g\in\{a,b,c\}$, $$\label{eq:rotation-gradient-bound} \bigl\langle \|X((df)g)\|^2\bigr\rangle \le\beta\\|df\\|_2^2.$$ For every complex edge form $h$, $$\label{eq:rotation-R-bound} R(h,\bar h) =-\bigl\langle H_2(hc,\bar h c)+\|X(hc)\|^2\bigr\rangle \le\beta\\|h\\|_2^2.$$ Moreover, adding $df$ in either argument of $R$ does not change its value whenever $f$ vanishes on $P$.
  - Explanation: A rotation potential that vanishes at pinned vertices has a controlled current second moment. The response form has an upper bound on conjugate arguments and is unchanged by adding such gradients; positive semidefiniteness is not asserted.
- **claim-fourth-order-family-response** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-fourth)): Let $(f_i)_{i\in I}$ be a finite family of complex vertex functions vanishing on $P$, and put $$u_i=df_i,\qquad T_{el}=\sum_{i\in I}u_i(e)\overline{u_i(l)},\qquad Q=\sum_{i\in I}S(f_i,\bar f_i).$$ Then $$\label{eq:rotation-family-bound} \begin{split} R(Q,\bar Q) &\le K_{\mathrm{rot}}(\beta)\\|T\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\\ &\quad+\beta\sum_{i,j\in I} \min\bigl\{\\|f_i\\|_\infty^2\\|df_j\\|_2^2, \\|f_j\\|_\infty^2\\|df_i\\|_2^2\bigr\}, \end{split}$$ where $$\label{eq:rotation-explicit-constant} K_{\mathrm{rot}}(\beta)=6\beta^2+4\beta^{3/2}+\beta.$$ In particular, the constants are independent of the graph, the family size, and the values of the fixed spins.
  - Explanation: The collective commutator response is bounded by the Hilbert–Schmidt cost of the gradient family and a symmetric minimum of cross costs. The explicit coefficient is independent of graph size, family size and the prescribed spins.
- **claim-vanishing-annular-twist-cost** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-annulus)): For every finite $\beta>0$ there is a finite constant $C_\beta$ such that, for all $N\geq2$ and all graphs and strengths just described, $$\label{eq:annulus-minmax} \frac{\min_{t\in\mathbb R}Z(t)}{\max_{t\in\mathbb R}Z(t)} \geq 1-\frac{C_\beta}{\log N}.$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:annulus-ratio} \frac{Z(\pi)}{Z(0)}\geq1-\frac{C_\beta}{\log N}.$$ The constant is independent of the subgraph, the strength array, and the twist parameter.
  - Explanation: The minimum annular boundary partition function approaches its maximum relative to that maximum. The estimate is uniform under deleting vertices or edges and weakening admissible strengths.
- **claim-multiscale-annular-family** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-modes)): There is a constant $C$, depending only on the fixed profiles, with the following properties for every $N\geq2$ and every such subgraph. First, $$\label{eq:annulus-commutator} \sum_i S(f_i,\overline{f_i})=-i\lambda_N\widetilde h, \qquad \lambda_N =N\!\!\sum_{\substack{1\leq r_p\leq N\\p_1>0}} r_p^{-3}\sin\left(\frac{2\pi p_1}{16N}\right) \geq\frac12\sum_{r=1}^N\frac1r.$$ Second, $$\label{eq:annulus-midpoint} \\|h\\|_2\leq C,\qquad \\|h+\widetilde h\\|_\infty\leq\frac CN,\qquad \\|h-\widetilde h\\|_1\leq\frac CN.$$ Finally, $$\label{eq:annulus-test-cost} \\|T\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2+ \sum_{i,j}\min\left\{ \\|f_i\\|_\infty^2\\|df_j\\|_2^2, \\|f_j\\|_\infty^2\\|df_i\\|_2^2 \right\} \leq C(1+\log N).$$ All edge norms use counting measure on $E$.
  - Explanation: The signed frequency family produces a collective commutator aligned with an approximation to the boundary twist. A harmonic lower bound amplifies the response, while the approximation error and the total family costs remain controlled after restriction to any retained subgraph.
- **claim-product-density-association** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-association)): Let $w$ be a positive log-supermodular density on a finite product of compact intervals or finite chains, with respect to the product of Lebesgue or counting measures. In the interval case, suppose $w$ is continuous. The probability law proportional to $w$ is associated. Moreover, its conditional law on all but one coordinate increases stochastically as the remaining coordinate increases.
  - Explanation: A positive log-supermodular density on the specified finite product yields association. Its conditional law on the remaining coordinates increases stochastically with the coordinate being conditioned on.
- **claim-free-edge-energy-inequalities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-correlation-inequalities)): In either the free Ising or the free planar system on a finite graph with nonnegative couplings, every edge energy has nonnegative mean, and any two edge energies have nonnegative covariance.
  - Explanation: Free Ising and planar edge energies have nonnegative means and pairwise nonnegative covariances under nonnegative couplings. The result does not state these inequalities for the full vector-spin law.
- **claim-free-amplitude-association** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-amplitude-association)): The amplitude law with density <a href="#eq:amplitude-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:amplitude-density">[eq:amplitude-density]</a> is associated.
  - Explanation: The amplitude marginal of the free three-component spin law is associated. The proof uses the separate Ising and planar partition functions and their edge-energy correlation inequalities.
- **claim-joint-amplitude-bond-association** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-joint-association)): The joint free law of $r$ and $\eta$ is associated, with both amplitudes and bond indicators ordered coordinatewise.
  - Explanation: Amplitudes and bond indicators are jointly associated under coordinatewise order. Conditional bond association and monotonicity combine with amplitude association through the total covariance identity.
- **claim-simultaneous-pinning-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-pinning)): For the three-component free spin law on any finite graph with nonnegative strengths, and bonds sampled by <a href="#eq:bond-parameter" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bond-parameter">[eq:bond-parameter]</a>, pinning any set of spins to $s_*$ cannot decrease the probability of any increasing bond event $A$.
  - Explanation: Pinning an arbitrary vertex set to the specified pole can only increase the probability of an increasing bond event. The comparison constrains both amplitudes and cluster signs.
- **claim-disjoint-annulus-crossing-suppression** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-crossings)): For each $\beta<\infty$ there are numbers $p_N=p_N(\beta)>0$ tending to zero as $N\to\infty$ with the following property. For every finite lattice subgraph, every strength array in $[0,\beta]$, and every list of centers whose closed boxes of sup radius $2N$ are pairwise disjoint, the free probability that all the corresponding annuli are crossed is at most $p_N$ to the number of annuli. The boxes need not lie inside the finite graph.
  - Explanation: The probability of simultaneous crossings of annuli with disjoint outer boxes is bounded by a product of uniformly small factors. Truncation by the finite graph does not invalidate the bound.
- **claim-exponential-bond-connectivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-connection)): For every finite $\beta>0$ there are constants $A_0<\infty$ and $m_0>0$, depending only on $\beta$, such that the three-component free bond law on every finite square-lattice subgraph with strengths in $[0,\beta]$ satisfies $$\mathbb P(x\leftrightarrow y) \leq A_0\exp(-m_0\\|x-y\\|_2) \qquad(x,y\in V).$$
  - Explanation: The embedded three-component free bond law has exponentially decaying connection probabilities, uniformly over finite square-lattice subgraphs and bounded nonnegative strengths.
- **claim-three-component-conditional-law** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-conditioning)): Let $n>3$, and let $\sigma_x\in S^{n-1}$ have the free Gibbs law <a href="#eq:model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model">[eq:model]</a> on a finite graph. Write $\sigma_x=(\rho_x s_x,v_x)$, with $v_x\in\mathbb R^{n-3}$, $\rho_x=\sqrt{1-\|v_x\|^2}$, and $s_x\in S^2$ whenever $\rho_x>0$. Conditional on $(v_x)_{x\in V}$, the directions $(s_x)_{x\in V}$ have exactly the free three-component Gibbs law with strengths $b_{xy}\rho_x\rho_y\in[0,\beta]$.
  - Explanation: After conditioning on the coordinates beyond the three-component block, the remaining spherical directions have the free three-component Gibbs law. The projected amplitudes weaken each original coupling, so the uniform three-component estimate applies conditionally.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gradient",
        "exact_quote": "If $f:V\\to\\mathbb C$ vanishes on $P$, then, for $g\\in\\{a,b,c\\}$, $$\\label{eq:rotation-gradient-bound}\n\\bigl\\langle |X((df)g)|^2\\bigr\\rangle\n\\le\\beta\\|df\\|_2^2.$$ For every complex edge form $h$, $$\\label{eq:rotation-R-bound}\nR(h,\\bar h)\n=-\\bigl\\langle H_2(hc,\\bar h c)+|X(hc)|^2\\bigr\\rangle\n\\le\\beta\\|h\\|_2^2.$$ Moreover, adding $df$ in either argument of $R$ does not change its value whenever $f$ vanishes on $P$.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gradient",
          "exact_quote": "If $f:V\\to\\mathbb C$ vanishes on $P$, then, for $g\\in\\{a,b,c\\}$, $$\\label{eq:rotation-gradient-bound}\n\\bigl\\langle |X((df)g)|^2\\bigr\\rangle\n\\le\\beta\\|df\\|_2^2.$$ For every complex edge form $h$, $$\\label{eq:rotation-R-bound}\nR(h,\\bar h)\n=-\\bigl\\langle H_2(hc,\\bar h c)+|X(hc)|^2\\bigr\\rangle\n\\le\\beta\\|h\\|_2^2.$$ Moreover, adding $df$ in either argument of $R$ does not change its value whenever $f$ vanishes on $P$.",
          "source_id": "PM-215.5"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-fourth",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(f_i)_{i\\in I}$ be a finite family of complex vertex functions vanishing on $P$, and put $$u_i=df_i,\\qquad\nT_{el}=\\sum_{i\\in I}u_i(e)\\overline{u_i(l)},\\qquad\nQ=\\sum_{i\\in I}S(f_i,\\bar f_i).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:rotation-family-bound}\n\\begin{split}\nR(Q,\\bar Q)\n&\\le K_{\\mathrm{rot}}(\\beta)\\|T\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\\\\n&\\quad+\\beta\\sum_{i,j\\in I}\n\\min\\bigl\\{\\|f_i\\|_\\infty^2\\|df_j\\|_2^2,\n             \\|f_j\\|_\\infty^2\\|df_i\\|_2^2\\bigr\\},\n\\end{split}$$ where $$\\label{eq:rotation-explicit-constant}\nK_{\\mathrm{rot}}(\\beta)=6\\beta^2+4\\beta^{3/2}+\\beta.$$ In particular, the constants are independent of the graph, the family size, and the values of the fixed spins.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-fourth",

          "source_id": "PM-215.5"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $(f_i)_{i\\in I}$ be a finite family of complex vertex functions vanishing on $P$, and put $$u_i=df_i,\\qquad\nT_{el}=\\sum_{i\\in I}u_i(e)\\overline{u_i(l)},\\qquad\nQ=\\sum_{i\\in I}S(f_i,\\bar f_i).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:rotation-family-bound}\n\\begin{split}\nR(Q,\\bar Q)\n&\\le K_{\\mathrm{rot}}(\\beta)\\|T\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\\\\n&\\quad+\\beta\\sum_{i,j\\in I}\n\\min\\bigl\\{\\|f_i\\|_\\infty^2\\|df_j\\|_2^2,\n             \\|f_j\\|_\\infty^2\\|df_i\\|_2^2\\bigr\\},\n\\end{split}$$ where $$\\label{eq:rotation-explicit-constant}\nK_{\\mathrm{rot}}(\\beta)=6\\beta^2+4\\beta^{3/2}+\\beta.$$ In particular, the constants are independent of the graph, the family size, and the values of the fixed spins.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-annulus",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite $\\beta>0$ there is a finite constant $C_\\beta$ such that, for all $N\\geq2$ and all graphs and strengths just described, $$\\label{eq:annulus-minmax}\n \\frac{\\min_{t\\in\\mathbb R}Z(t)}{\\max_{t\\in\\mathbb R}Z(t)}\n \\geq 1-\\frac{C_\\beta}{\\log N}.$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:annulus-ratio}\n \\frac{Z(\\pi)}{Z(0)}\\geq1-\\frac{C_\\beta}{\\log N}.$$ The constant is independent of the subgraph, the strength array, and the twist parameter.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-modes",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C$, depending only on the fixed profiles, with the following properties for every $N\\geq2$ and every such subgraph. First, $$\\label{eq:annulus-commutator}\n \\sum_i S(f_i,\\overline{f_i})=-i\\lambda_N\\widetilde h,\n \\qquad\n \\lambda_N\n =N\\!\\!\\sum_{\\substack{1\\leq r_p\\leq N\\\\p_1>0}}\n      r_p^{-3}\\sin\\left(\\frac{2\\pi p_1}{16N}\\right)\n \\geq\\frac12\\sum_{r=1}^N\\frac1r.$$ Second, $$\\label{eq:annulus-midpoint}\n \\|h\\|_2\\leq C,\\qquad\n \\|h+\\widetilde h\\|_\\infty\\leq\\frac CN,\\qquad\n \\|h-\\widetilde h\\|_1\\leq\\frac CN.$$ Finally, $$\\label{eq:annulus-test-cost}\n \\|T\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2+\n \\sum_{i,j}\\min\\left\\{\n   \\|f_i\\|_\\infty^2\\|df_j\\|_2^2,\n   \\|f_j\\|_\\infty^2\\|df_i\\|_2^2\n \\right\\}\n \\leq C(1+\\log N).$$ All edge norms use counting measure on $E$.",
        "source_id": "PM-215.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-modes",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Free spherical Gibbs law** ([source](#eq:model)): Write $$\mathrm d\mu_{G,b}^{(n)}(\sigma) =\frac{1}{Z_{G,b}^{(n)}} \exp\!\left(\sum_{\{x,y\}\in E}b_{xy}\sigma_x\cdot\sigma_y\right) \prod_{x\in V}\mathrm d\omega_{n-1}(\sigma_x), \label{eq:model}$$ where $\omega_{n-1}$ is uniform probability measure on the unit sphere.
- **Free-law expectation** ([source](#eq:model)): Expectations are denoted by $\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle_{G,b}^{(n)}$.
- **Twisted interaction and normalized partition response** ([source](#eq:rotation-partition)): For an edge field $A=(A_e)_{e\in E}$ of skew matrices, put $$\label{eq:rotation-partition} \begin{split} H(A)&=\sum_{e=(x,y)}b_e s_x^T\bigl(\exp(A_e)-I\bigr)s_y,\\ W(A)&=\bigl\langle\exp H(A)\bigr\rangle. \end{split}$$
- **Discrete vertex gradient** ([source](#eq:rotation-local-coefficients)): For a scalar function on vertices, define $df(e)=f_y-f_x$.
- **Hilbert–Schmidt edge-matrix norm** ([source](#eq:rotation-local-coefficients)): For a matrix indexed by edges, $\\|T\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=\sum_{e,l\in E}\|T_{el}\|^2$.
- **Antisymmetric vertex-pair edge form** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For vertex functions $f,g$, write $$\label{eq:rotation-S-definition} S(f,g)_e=\tfrac12(f_xg_y-g_xf_y),\qquad e=(x,y).$$
- **Signed annular frequency modes** ([source](#eq:annulus-modes)): Introduce four groups of vertex functions, restricted to $V$: $$\label{eq:annulus-modes} f_{m,\pm,p}(x) =r_p^{-3/2}\phi_{m,\pm}(x/N) \exp\left(\frac{2\pi i\,p\cdot x}{16N}\right), \qquad 1\leq r_p\leq N,\quad \pm p_m>0.$$
- **Amplitude-sign-angle spin decomposition** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Write $$\label{eq:spin-disintegration} s_x=(r_x\tau_x,q_x\cos\theta_x,q_x\sin\theta_x), \qquad q_x=(1-r_x^2)^{1/2}.$$
- **Amplitude-conditioned Ising and planar couplings** ([source](#pm-h-13)): Conditional on all the amplitudes $r=(r_x)_{x\in V}$, the signs and angles are independent Gibbs systems with respective couplings $$\label{eq:amplitude-couplings} K_{xy}=b_{xy}r_xr_y, \qquad L_{xy}=b_{xy}q_xq_y.$$
- **Association** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A probability law on a coordinatewise ordered product is *associated* if the covariance of any two bounded increasing functions is nonnegative.
- **Log-supermodularity** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A positive function $w$ is *log-supermodular* if $$w(u\vee v)w(u\wedge v)\geq w(u)w(v),$$ where join and meet are taken coordinatewise.
- **Pinned spin law** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For any set $B\subseteq V$, the pinned spin law means that $s_x=s_*$ for $x\in B$, while all other spins retain uniform reference measures and the same edge interactions.
- **Annulus crossing** ([source](#pm-h-14)): An *annulus crossing* is an open path in the restriction of $G$ to $D_N(z)$, joining its layers of radii $N$ and $2N$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-uniform-spin-correlation-envelope | \langle\sigma_x\cdot\sigma_y\rangle_{G,b}^{(n)} | ≤ | $A\exp(-m\\|x-y\\|_2)$ | The constants depend on spin dimension and inverse temperature, uniformly over the finite subgraph and strength array. |
| q-spin-correlation-nonnegativity | \langle\sigma_x\cdot\sigma_y\rangle_{G,b}^{(n)} | ≥ | $0$ | All admissible vertex pairs and strength arrays. |
| q-gradient-current-second-moment | \bigl\langle \|X((df)g)\|^2\bigr\rangle | ≤ | $\beta\\|df\\|_2^2$ | Complex vertex potentials vanishing on the prescribed set. |
| q-edge-response-upper-bound | R(h,\bar h) | ≤ | $\beta\\|h\\|_2^2$ | All complex edge forms; no positivity of the response form is required. |
| q-explicit-rotation-coefficient | K_{\mathrm{rot}}(\beta) | = | $6\beta^2+4\beta^{3/2}+\beta$ | Only the strength ceiling enters the explicit coefficient. |
| q-annular-minimum-maximum-ratio | \frac{\min_{t\in\mathbb R}Z(t)}{\max_{t\in\mathbb R}Z(t)} | ≥ | $1-\frac{C_\beta}{\log N}$ | Uniform over retained vertices, edges, strengths and twist parameter. |
| q-opposite-boundary-partition-ratio | \frac{Z(\pi)}{Z(0)} | ≥ | $1-\frac{C_\beta}{\log N}$ | The same uniform annular constant applies. |
| q-harmonic-commutator-amplification | \lambda_N | ≥ | $\frac12\sum_{r=1}^N\frac1r$ | The amplification depends on the annular scale, independently of retained edges. |
| q-annular-form-approximation-error | \\|h-\widetilde h\\|_1 | ≤ | $\frac CN$ | The constant depends only on fixed smooth profiles. |
| q-unnormalized-fourier-operator-norm | \\|F\\|_{\mathrm{op}} | = | $16N$ | The torus size and selected distinct frequency columns determine the norm. |
| q-coarse-path-convergence-factor | \alpha | = | $4p^{1/25}$ | A sufficiently large finite scale is selected from the crossing-suppression sequence. |
| q-coarse-path-length-lower-bound | k | ≥ | $d/N-1$ | Endpoint sup distance and the selected coarse scale. |
| q-bond-exponential-decay-rate | m_0 | = | $\frac{-\log\alpha}{\sqrt2\,N}$ | The selected finite scale and crossing bound depend only on inverse temperature. |
| q-spin-prefactor-from-bond-prefactor | A | = | $nA_0$ | Spin dimension multiplies the three-component bond prefactor. |
| q-spin-rate-from-bond-rate | m | = | $m_0$ | The uniform three-component rate survives the conditional reduction. |

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-modes",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C$, depending only on the fixed profiles, with the following properties for every $N\\geq2$ and every such subgraph. First, $$\\label{eq:annulus-commutator}\n \\sum_i S(f_i,\\overline{f_i})=-i\\lambda_N\\widetilde h,\n \\qquad\n \\lambda_N\n =N\\!\\!\\sum_{\\substack{1\\leq r_p\\leq N\\\\p_1>0}}\n      r_p^{-3}\\sin\\left(\\frac{2\\pi p_1}{16N}\\right)\n \\geq\\frac12\\sum_{r=1}^N\\frac1r.$$ Second, $$\\label{eq:annulus-midpoint}\n \\|h\\|_2\\leq C,\\qquad\n \\|h+\\widetilde h\\|_\\infty\\leq\\frac CN,\\qquad\n \\|h-\\widetilde h\\|_1\\leq\\frac CN.$$ Finally, $$\\label{eq:annulus-test-cost}\n \\|T\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2+\n \\sum_{i,j}\\min\\left\\{\n   \\|f_i\\|_\\infty^2\\|df_j\\|_2^2,\n   \\|f_j\\|_\\infty^2\\|df_i\\|_2^2\n \\right\\}\n \\leq C(1+\\log N).$$ All edge norms use counting measure on $E$.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:annulus-fourier-norm",
          "exact_quote": "It remains to bound $T$. Applying the triangle inequality mode by mode would give only $\\|T\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}\\leq\\sum_i\\|df_i\\|_2^2=O(N)$; we use Fourier orthogonality for the sharper bound. Work first in a single profile group and let $U$ be the matrix whose $p$-th column is $r_p^{1/2}df_p$. Embed the annulus in the discrete torus $Q_N=(\\mathbb Z/(16N)\\mathbb Z)^2$. Its representatives may be chosen in $[-8N,8N-1]^2$; all profiles vanish near the boundary of this square, so they also define torus functions. Let $F$ have columns $$F_{x,p}=\\exp\\left(\\frac{2\\pi i\\,p\\cdot x}{16N}\\right),\n \\qquad x\\in Q_N,$$ using the frequencies of this group. They are distinct modulo $16N$, and orthogonality of these unnormalized waves gives $$\\label{eq:annulus-fourier-norm}\n F^*F=(16N)^2I,\\qquad \\|F\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}=16N.$$ For direction $j$, let $P_j$ restrict functions on $Q_N$ to the origins of the coordinate-$j$ edges in $E$. Let $M_v$ denote multiplication by $v$ on $Q_N$, and set $$\\Delta_j\\phi(x)=\\phi((x+e_j)/N)-\\phi(x/N),\n \\qquad \\phi^{(j)}(x)=\\phi((x+e_j)/N).$$ The directional block of $U$ has the exact factorization $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{eq:annulus-operator-factorization}\n U_j={}&P_jM_{\\Delta_j\\phi}F\n              \\operatorname{diag}(r_p^{-1})\\notag\\\\\n &+P_jM_{\\phi^{(j)}}F\n   \\operatorname{diag}\\left(\n      \\frac{\\exp(2\\pi i p_j/(16N))-1}{r_p}\\right).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\|P_j\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\leq1$, $\\|\\Delta_j\\phi\\|_\\infty\\leq C/N$, and the last diagonal factor has operator norm at most $C/N$, since $|p_j|\\leq r_p$. Equation <a href=\"#eq:annulus-fourier-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:annulus-fourier-norm\">[eq:annulus-fourier-norm]</a> now gives $\\|U_j\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\leq C$ and hence $\\|U\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\leq C$ after combining the two directions.",
          "source_id": "PM-215.5"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-unnormalized-fourier-operator-norm",
      "kind": "constant",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "\\|F\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "Unnormalized torus Fourier columns for a fixed profile group.",

          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{eq:coarse-path-alpha}\n 0<p:=p_N<1,\n \\qquad \\alpha:=4p^{1/25}<1.$$ Partition $\\mathbb Z^2$ into boxes $z+\\{0,\\ldots,N-1\\}^2$, with coarse vertices $z\\in N\\mathbb Z^2$. Suppose $0$ and $y$ are joined by an open path and $d:=\\|y\\|_\\infty>4N$. Recording the visited boxes and erasing loops produces a simple nearest-neighbor coarse path from the origin’s box to the box containing $y$. If its length is $k$, then $$\\label{eq:coarse-path-length}\n k\\geq d/N-1.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-215.5"
        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-gradient-gauge-identities** [claim](#stmt-lem-gradient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gradient): Apply real local changes of spin variables, differentiate the constant partition response, and extend the resulting identities complex linearly. -> Current second-moment control, an upper bound for the response form and its gradient invariance.
- **step-fourth-order-family-contraction** [claim](#stmt-prop-fourth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fourth): Use the noncommuting gauge expansion to express the collective response through fourth derivatives and cross terms. Retain a nonnegative square and bound the remaining contractions by gradient estimates. -> The fourth-order family inequality with its explicit coefficient.
- **step-multiscale-frequency-construction** [claim](#stmt-lem-modes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-modes): Sum signed frequency modes, use centered Taylor expansion for the boundary-form approximation, and apply torus Fourier orthogonality to control the family matrix. -> Harmonic commutator amplification, controlled approximation error and logarithmic collective costs.
- **step-boundary-twist-curvature** [claim](#stmt-prop-annulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-modes-2): Apply the family inequality in each twisted law, transfer the upper response bound to the true boundary form without assuming positivity, then integrate the curvature inequality from a maximum of the periodic partition function. -> Uniform minimum-to-maximum and opposite-boundary partition-ratio bounds.
- **step-finite-product-association** [claim](#stmt-lem-association) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-association): Induct on the product coordinates. Use an increasing likelihood-ratio tilt for conditional stochastic order and the total covariance decomposition for association. -> Association and conditional stochastic monotonicity.
- **step-edge-energy-correlation-inequalities** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-correlation-inequalities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-correlation-inequalities): Expand the free Gibbs exponential for the means. For covariances, double the system and use either squared planar reference integrals or nonnegative Ising reference moments. -> Nonnegative edge-energy means and pairwise covariances.
- **step-amplitude-log-supermodularity** [claim](#stmt-lem-amplitude-association) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-amplitude-association): Differentiate the logarithms of the conditioned Ising and planar partition functions. Their sign structure makes distinct-site mixed derivatives nonnegative; continuity extends the lattice inequality to the closed amplitude cube. -> Association of the free amplitude marginal.
- **step-joint-amplitude-bond-association** [claim](#stmt-lem-joint-association) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-joint-association): Prove conditional random-cluster association and stochastic increase in the bond parameters, handle forced-closed edges, then combine conditional association with amplitude association. -> Joint coordinatewise association of amplitudes and bonds.
- **step-pinning-by-positive-cap-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-pinning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pinning): Condition on positive caps at the selected vertices. The induced amplitude-bond tilt is increasing, so joint association gives the comparison; continuity then passes to exact pinning. -> Stochastic comparison for simultaneous pinning to the pole.
- **step-disjoint-annulus-crossing-product** [claim](#stmt-prop-crossings) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-crossings): Identify a pinned annulus crossing with the partition-ratio deficit. Pin all retained boundary layers simultaneously, use nearest-neighbor separation to obtain independent annulus interiors and multiply their crossing bounds. -> Uniform product suppression of simultaneous annulus crossings, including truncated boxes.
- **step-coarse-path-exponential-connectivity** [claim](#stmt-prop-connection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-connection): Select a finite scale with sufficiently small crossing probability. Coarse-grain a connecting path, erase loops, select a disjoint residue class of annuli and sum the resulting geometric path bound. -> Uniform exponential bond-connectivity decay.
- **step-spherical-conditional-projection** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditioning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditioning): Represent a uniform sphere point by normalized independent Gaussian blocks. The direction of the three-component block remains independent of the conditioned coordinates; remove the conditional constant interaction from the normalization. -> A free three-component conditional Gibbs law with weakened strengths.
- **step-spin-decay-from-weighted-connectivity** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditioning-2): Bound the amplitude-weighted connection identity by bond connectivity. Apply the same estimate conditionally in higher spin dimension, average the amplitude factors and use coordinate-permutation symmetry. -> Nonnegative exponentially decaying full spin correlations.
- **step-free-boundary-limit-passage** [claim](#stmt-cor-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditioning-2): Use volume-independent constants for the limsup. Along a locally weakly convergent subsequence, pass the bounded continuous local spin product to its limiting expectation. -> The same spin-correlation bound in every specified subsequential limit.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-uniform-spin-decay**: Spin two-point correlations are nonnegative and decay exponentially, uniformly over finite nearest-neighbor square-lattice subgraphs and bounded ferromagnetic strength arrays. The constants may depend on the spin dimension and inverse temperature.
- **invariant-annular-twist-suppression**: The minimum-to-maximum ratio of the annular boundary partition function approaches unity with a logarithmic error, uniformly under deleting vertices or edges and weakening the retained strengths.
- **invariant-joint-association**: Bounded increasing functions of amplitudes and bond indicators have nonnegative covariance under the joint free law.
- **invariant-pinning-monotonicity**: Simultaneously pinning an arbitrary set of spins to the common pole cannot decrease the probability of an increasing bond event.
- **invariant-disjoint-crossing-bound**: The probability of simultaneous crossings of annuli with pairwise disjoint closed outer boxes is bounded by the product of a common scale-dependent upper bound tending to zero.
- **invariant-conditional-reduction**: Conditioning on the coordinates after the third leaves a free three-component Gibbs law whose strengths remain in the original allowed interval.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-planar-extension**: Apply the all-temperature exponential spin bound to the two-component planar rotator. -> The polynomial lower bound in the low-temperature planar regime rules out the proposed exponential upper bound.
- **anti-full-transfer-gap**: Interpret exponential spin decay as a full transfer gap controlling every local observable. -> The result does not establish covariance decay for rotation-invariant bond energies or all other local observables.
- **anti-temperature-asymptotics**: Extract a low-temperature correlation-length asymptotic or a temperature-uniform positive decay rate. -> The constants may deteriorate as inverse temperature grows, and the correlation-length asymptotic is not identified.
- **anti-gibbs-uniqueness**: Infer uniqueness of infinite-volume Gibbs states from the free-boundary correlation estimate. -> Passing a bound to subsequential local weak limits does not identify those limits or prove uniqueness.
- **anti-positive-response-form**: Apply positive-form inequalities directly to the bilinear rotation response. -> The response is symmetric but is not assumed positive semidefinite; positivity-based comparison would insert an unsupported premise.
- **anti-unpinned-crossing-independence**: Multiply free crossing probabilities for disjoint annuli by assuming their crossing events are independent. -> The proof obtains independence after simultaneous boundary pinning; disjoint geometry alone does not establish independence under the free spin law.
- **anti-general-graph-decay**: Extend the final exponential connection and spin bounds to arbitrary finite graphs, long-range interactions, or other lattice geometries. -> Finite-graph association and rotation identities do not supply the square-annulus separation and coarse-path argument outside the stated lattice setting.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal result is an existential exponential bound for continuous sphere-spin integrals over every finite nearest-neighbor square-lattice subgraph and every allowed strength array, followed by assertions about infinite-volume limits. A finite collection of evaluations cannot decide those universal claims. The proof also chooses a sufficiently large annulus scale through a vanishing crossing sequence rather than providing an executable numerical bound for the required scale. Source obstruction: stmt-thm-main asserts the existence of temperature-dependent constants uniformly over graphs and strengths; stmt-cor-limits passes the estimate to subsequential local weak limits; proof-stmt-prop-connection chooses a finite integer scale sufficiently large for the crossing bound. The finite algebraic constructions inside the proof do not constitute a finite decision procedure for these conclusions.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniform correlation estimate supplies a mathematical property of a Gibbs law, but no procedure for computing its correlations or numerical decay constants. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Spatial decay of equilibrium bond connectivity does not establish convergence of a simulation, equilibration times, or mixing of a spin-update process. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The annular partition-function comparison is an analytic bound with an unspecified temperature-dependent constant, rather than a numerical partition-function estimation tool. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The paper gives a concrete conditional bond construction: with spins fixed, independently open equal-sign edges using the stated amplitude-dependent probability. This supports adapting a bond-generation stage, while leaving generation of the equilibrium spin input unresolved. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The conditional bond weight uses the number of open components, including singletons, but the paper does not construct a component-maintenance data structure or establish its computational costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Fourier orthogonality controls a matrix norm in the multiscale proof; it does not specify an accelerator implementation, memory layout, or hardware performance contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The stochastic comparison concerns increasing bond events under spin pinning, with no construction of a security primitive or adversarial security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Association establishes nonnegative covariance for increasing functions under a specified equilibrium law, but supplies no learning objective, estimator, or training procedure. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The fourth-order family inequality bounds a rotation response, and its bilinear form is explicitly not assumed positive semidefinite; no optimisation solver or convexity guarantee follows. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The product bound for annulus crossings requires pairwise disjoint outer boxes and a pinning argument. It is not a construction of independent distributed processes or a protocol guarantee. |
| other (Classical statistical mechanics) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Conditioning the remaining spin coordinates constructs a free three-component Gibbs law with weakened edge strengths. The uniform correlation estimate can then be transferred through conditional expectation without assumptions on the distribution of those strengths. |

          "exact_quote": "We now use the embedded Ising representation underlying Wolff’s reflection clusters (Wolff 1989) [[Wolff](#ref-Wolff)], coupled to bonds as in Edwards–Sokal (Edwards and Sokal 1988) [[EdwardsSokal](#ref-EdwardsSokal)]. Given the spins, sample bonds $\\eta_e\\in\\{0,1\\}$ independently. For $e=\\{x,y\\}$, set $$\\label{eq:bond-parameter}\n p_e=1-e^{-2K_e},\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb P(\\eta_e=1\\mid s)\n   =p_e\\mathbf1_{\\{\\tau_x=\\tau_y\\}}.$$ The elementary identity $$\\label{eq:ising-bond-expansion}\n e^{K_e\\tau_x\\tau_y}\n  =e^{K_e}\\big[(1-p_e)+p_e\\mathbf1_{\\{\\tau_x=\\tau_y\\}}\\big]$$ shows that, conditional on $r,\\eta$, the signs are independent fair signs on the open connected components. In particular, we obtain the weighted connection identity of Campbell and Chayes (Campbell and Chayes 1998, Corollary 4, Equation (7)) [[CC](#ref-CC)]: $$\\label{eq:spin-connection}\n \\langle s_x^1s_y^1\\rangle_{G,b}^{(3)}\n =\\mathbb E\\big[r_xr_y\\mathbf1_{\\{x\\leftrightarrow y\\}}\\big],$$ where the connection uses only open edges of $G$.",

Indeed, the first coordinate of a uniform point on $S^2$ is uniform on $[-1,1]$, independently of its azimuthal angle. Conditional on all the amplitudes $r=(r_x)_{x\\in V}$, the signs and angles are independent Gibbs systems with respective couplings $$\\label{eq:amplitude-couplings}\n K_{xy}=b_{xy}r_xr_y,\n \\qquad L_{xy}=b_{xy}q_xq_y.$$ Their edge energies are $\\tau_x\\tau_y$ and $\\cos(\\theta_x-\\theta_y)$. Let $Z_I(K)$ and $Z_P(L)$ denote their partition functions, using uniform probability reference measures. The amplitude density is therefore proportional to $$\\label{eq:amplitude-density}\n \\rho(r)=Z_I(K(r))Z_P(L(r)).$$",
          "source_object": "eq:spin-disintegration"
        }

The function $k$ is supermodular: the change on opening an edge is $-1$ if its endpoints were disconnected and $0$ otherwise, and this change increases as other bonds are opened. Hence the weight is log-supermodular and its law is associated by Lemma <a href=\"#lem:association\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:association\">7</a>. Increasing any nondegenerate parameter $p_e$ tilts the weight by an increasing likelihood ratio. Association therefore implies stochastic increase of the conditional bond law in each $p_e$, and consequently in $r$.           "exact_quote": "It remains to bound $T$. Applying the triangle inequality mode by mode would give only $\\|T\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}\\leq\\sum_i\\|df_i\\|_2^2=O(N)$; we use Fourier orthogonality for the sharper bound. Work first in a single profile group and let $U$ be the matrix whose $p$-th column is $r_p^{1/2}df_p$. Embed the annulus in the discrete torus $Q_N=(\\mathbb Z/(16N)\\mathbb Z)^2$. Its representatives may be chosen in $[-8N,8N-1]^2$; all profiles vanish near the boundary of this square, so they also define torus functions. Let $F$ have columns $$F_{x,p}=\\exp\\left(\\frac{2\\pi i\\,p\\cdot x}{16N}\\right),\n \\qquad x\\in Q_N,$$ using the frequencies of this group. They are distinct modulo $16N$, and orthogonality of these unnormalized waves gives $$\\label{eq:annulus-fourier-norm}\n F^*F=(16N)^2I,\\qquad \\|F\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}=16N.$$ For direction $j$, let $P_j$ restrict functions on $Q_N$ to the origins of the coordinate-$j$ edges in $E$. Let $M_v$ denote multiplication by $v$ on $Q_N$, and set $$\\Delta_j\\phi(x)=\\phi((x+e_j)/N)-\\phi(x/N),\n \\qquad \\phi^{(j)}(x)=\\phi((x+e_j)/N).$$ The directional block of $U$ has the exact factorization $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{eq:annulus-operator-factorization}\n U_j={}&P_jM_{\\Delta_j\\phi}F\n              \\operatorname{diag}(r_p^{-1})\\notag\\\\\n &+P_jM_{\\phi^{(j)}}F\n   \\operatorname{diag}\\left(\n      \\frac{\\exp(2\\pi i p_j/(16N))-1}{r_p}\\right).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\|P_j\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\leq1$, $\\|\\Delta_j\\phi\\|_\\infty\\leq C/N$, and the last diagonal factor has operator norm at most $C/N$, since $|p_j|\\leq r_p$. Equation <a href=\"#eq:annulus-fourier-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:annulus-fourier-norm\">[eq:annulus-fourier-norm]</a> now gives $\\|U_j\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\leq C$ and hence $\\|U\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\leq C$ after combining the two directions.",

        }

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-spin-dimension** (hypothesis): The exponential spin-decay assertion requires integer spin dimension at least three; the low-temperature planar rotator cannot satisfy the same all-temperature assertion. Blocked use: ['Apply the main exponential upper bound to the two-component planar rotator.']
- **limit-square-lattice-geometry** (formal_scope): The final spin and connection decay bounds concern finite nearest-neighbor square-lattice subgraphs. The finite-graph rotation and association results do not extend that final geometric scope. Blocked use: ['Assert the same exponential decay on arbitrary graphs, other lattice geometries or long-range interaction networks.']
- **limit-free-pair-interaction-law** (hypothesis): The main theorem uses the free spherical Gibbs law with bounded nonnegative pair strengths, without an external field or additional interaction. Auxiliary pinned laws do not establish the main bound for arbitrary boundary prescriptions. Blocked use: ['Apply the main theorem directly to fixed boundary spins, signed couplings, external fields or additional interactions.']
- **limit-fixed-finite-temperature** (hypothesis): The decay constants are asserted at each finite positive inverse temperature. No positive decay rate uniform through the low-temperature limit is established. Blocked use: ['Use the theorem as a temperature-uniform positive mass estimate.', 'Apply the stated bound at infinite inverse temperature.']
- **limit-spin-observable-only** (formal_scope): Exponential spin two-point decay does not establish a full transfer gap or decay of covariances for all local observables. Blocked use: ['Infer exponential covariance decay for rotation-invariant bond energies.', 'Claim a full transfer gap from the spin bound.']
- **limit-asymptotics-and-uniqueness** (unknown): The paper does not identify the low-temperature correlation-length asymptotic or prove uniqueness of infinite-volume Gibbs states. Blocked use: ['Extract an exact low-temperature correlation-length prefactor.', 'Identify all infinite-volume Gibbs states from the free-boundary limit estimate.']
- **limit-gradient-boundary-vanishing** (hypothesis): The gradient current estimate and gradient invariance require vertex potentials vanishing at the prescribed vertices. The fourth-order family must satisfy the same boundary restriction. Blocked use: ['Remove a gradient from the response when its potential changes a pinned spin.', 'Apply the family inequality to test functions nonzero on the prescribed set.']
- **limit-response-without-positivity** (formal_scope): The response form is symmetric but is not assumed positive semidefinite. Transferring the tested bound to the boundary twist requires separate control of current differences and local quadratic errors. Blocked use: ['Apply a positive-form Cauchy–Schwarz inequality directly to the response.', 'Discard the approximation-error argument by assuming response positivity.']
- **limit-real-gauge-changes** (formal_scope): Complex multilinear derivative identities arise by extension from real rotations. They do not authorize complex changes of spin variables in the spherical integral. Blocked use: ['Treat the complex test functions as complex rotations preserving the original integration measure.']
- **limit-annular-profile-and-frequency-construction** (hypothesis): The multiscale estimates use fixed smooth profiles supported away from the pinned layers and the specified signed frequency family. Arbitrary profiles or frequency weights need separate cost and commutator estimates. Blocked use: ['Replace the annular family by arbitrary oscillatory tests while retaining its logarithmic gain.', 'Apply the family costs to profiles that fail the pinned-boundary condition.']
- **limit-fourier-orthogonality** (hypothesis): The Fourier matrix norm estimate uses distinct frequencies on the specified discrete torus. A modewise triangle bound does not provide the sharper collective cost needed by the annular argument. Blocked use: ['Reuse the Fourier norm identity with repeated frequency columns.', 'Replace the collective matrix estimate by a modewise triangle estimate without checking the resulting cost.']
- **limit-product-association-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The finite-product association criterion assumes a positive log-supermodular density on the stated compact interval or finite chain factors, with continuity in the interval case. Blocked use: ['Apply the criterion to an arbitrary probability density without proving log-supermodularity.', 'Ignore positivity or interval continuity when invoking the stated criterion.']
- **limit-restricted-edge-energy-inequalities** (formal_scope): The edge-energy correlation inequalities are proved for the free Ising and free planar systems with nonnegative couplings. They are not asserted for the full vector-spin law. Blocked use: ['Assume nonnegative covariances for arbitrary vector-spin observables.', 'Transfer the edge-energy inequalities to signed couplings or unspecified boundary conditions.']
- **limit-amplitude-and-bond-degeneracies** (edge_case): Singular amplitude derivatives at the endpoint do not invalidate the association proof because the density extends continuously. A zero bond parameter forces the corresponding edge closed. Blocked use: ['Require finite endpoint derivatives as a premise of amplitude association.', 'Use a nondegenerate likelihood-ratio formula on an edge whose opening parameter vanishes.']
- **limit-pinning-event-and-pole** (hypothesis): The pinning comparison concerns increasing bond events and simultaneous pinning to the specified common pole. Its proof uses joint amplitude-bond association and a continuous positive-cap limit. Blocked use: ['Claim the comparison for arbitrary bond events.', 'Replace common-pole pinning by unrelated boundary directions without a separate comparison.', 'Condition cluster signs alone and omit the amplitude constraint.']
- **limit-restricted-disjoint-annulus-crossings** (hypothesis): A crossing stays within the restricted annulus graph. The simultaneous product bound requires pairwise disjoint closed outer boxes and obtains independence after boundary pinning, rather than under the original free law. Blocked use: ['Count a path leaving the annulus as an annulus crossing.', 'Multiply free crossing probabilities by assuming independence.', 'Use the stated product bound for overlapping outer boxes.']
- **limit-qualitative-coarse-scale-selection** (nonconstructive): The connectivity proof chooses a sufficiently large finite annulus scale using the vanishing crossing sequence. It does not supply a numerical scale or numerical decay constants as functions of inverse temperature. Blocked use: ['Treat the existential decay rate as a computed numerical tolerance.', 'Claim an executable procedure for selecting the required scale solely from the qualitative convergence assertion.']
- **limit-conditional-free-spherical-reduction** (hypothesis): The conditional reduction starts from the free higher-component spherical Gibbs law and conditions on all coordinates beyond the retained block. Uniformity over weakened strength arrays is essential before averaging. Blocked use: ['Apply the same reduction to arbitrary spin reference measures or additional interactions.', 'Average a conditional estimate whose constants depend uncontrolledly on the projected environment.']
- **limit-zero-residual-amplitude** (edge_case): The residual direction need not be defined by division when its amplitude vanishes. Such sites have zero probability under the stated law; an auxiliary direction would decouple the site without changing spin products. Blocked use: ['Divide by a vanishing residual amplitude.', 'Treat the exceptional residual direction as an additional interacting degree of freedom.']
- **limit-specified-free-boundary-limits** (formal_scope): The infinite-volume conclusion passes the spin bound to subsequential local weak limits of the specified free-boundary square laws. It does not assert the same conclusion for every possible infinite-volume boundary construction. Blocked use: ['Replace the specified free-boundary sequence by arbitrary boundary laws without verification.', 'Infer a uniquely constructed infinite-volume state.']
- **limit-equilibrium-versus-mixing** (formal_scope): The equilibrium spin and bond-connectivity estimates do not establish convergence rates for a simulation update process. Blocked use: ['Claim rapid mixing or equilibration times from exponential spatial connectivity alone.']

Scope note of family 215 (`lean/docs/215.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Exponential decay in two-dimensional classical $O(n)$ models](../../preprints/Exponential-decay-in-two-dimensional-classical-On-models-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result proves exponential decay of spin correlations for the two-dimensional classical $O(n)$ model at every temperature when $n\ge3$. For each interaction bound $\beta>0$, constants $A$ and $m>0$ work for every finite square-lattice subgraph with free boundary and nonnegative edge strengths at most $\beta$: the correlation between sites $x,y$ is at most $Ae^{-m|x-y|}$. The infinite-volume limit and the separate $O(4)$ spectral-gap claim are not included.

Comparator source `ClassicalON.lean` (1961 bytes, sha256 `c2f5f88455b811eb6b56c1ae80ff91f4e398e18e7c7fbeee6daca8da4122d696`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
open MeasureTheory
open scoped BigOperators InnerProductSpace

namespace ClassicalON

abbrev Site := ℤ × ℤ

def PositiveNeighbor (x y : Site) : Prop :=
  (y.1 = x.1 + 1 ∧ y.2 = x.2) ∨ (y.1 = x.1 ∧ y.2 = x.2 + 1)

structure LatticeGraph where
  vertices : Finset Site
  edges : Finset (vertices × vertices)
  nearest : ∀ e ∈ edges, PositiveNeighbor e.1.val e.2.val

abbrev Spin (n : ℕ) := ↥(Metric.sphere (0 : EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n)) 1)
abbrev Configuration (n : ℕ) (G : LatticeGraph) := G.vertices → Spin n

def sphereProbability (n : ℕ) : Measure (Spin n) :=
  let surface := (volume : Measure (EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n))).toSphere
  (surface Set.univ)⁻¹ • surface

def referenceLaw (n : ℕ) (G : LatticeGraph) : Measure (Configuration n G) :=
  Measure.pi (fun _ => sphereProbability n)

def interaction (n : ℕ) (G : LatticeGraph) (b : G.edges → ℝ)
    (σ : Configuration n G) : ℝ :=
  ∑ e : G.edges, b e * ⟪(σ e.val.1).val, (σ e.val.2).val⟫_ℝ

def partition (n : ℕ) (G : LatticeGraph) (b : G.edges → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∫ σ, Real.exp (interaction n G b σ) ∂referenceLaw n G

def correlation (n : ℕ) (G : LatticeGraph) (b : G.edges → ℝ)
    (x y : G.vertices) : ℝ :=
  (∫ σ, ⟪(σ x).val, (σ y).val⟫_ℝ * Real.exp (interaction n G b σ)
    ∂referenceLaw n G) / partition n G b

def siteDistance (x y : Site) : ℝ :=
  Real.sqrt (((x.1 : ℝ) - (y.1 : ℝ)) ^ 2 + ((x.2 : ℝ) - (y.2 : ℝ)) ^ 2)

def ExponentialDecay : Prop :=
  ∀ (n : ℕ), 3 ≤ n → ∀ (β : ℝ), 0 < β →
    ∃ A m : ℝ, 0 < m ∧ ∀ (G : LatticeGraph) (b : G.edges → ℝ),
      (∀ e, 0 ≤ b e ∧ b e ≤ β) → ∀ x y : G.vertices,
        0 ≤ correlation n G b x y ∧
          correlation n G b x y ≤ A * Real.exp (-m * siteDistance x.val y.val)

theorem main : ExponentialDecay := by
  sorry

end ClassicalON

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove exponential decay of two-point correlations for the classical nearest-neighbor $`O(n)`$ model on the square lattice, for every <i>n</i> ≥ 3 and every finite positive inverse temperature. The estimate is uniform over finite free-boundary subgraphs and bounded nonnegative edge strengths. This resolves positively the all-temperature exponential spin-decay conjecture for these models.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
